## Author’s Note / 作者注

===Original===
The reissue of this book in a new form does not strictly speaking require another Preface. But since this is distinctly a place for personal remarks I take the opportunity to refer in this Author’s Note to two points arising from certain statements about myself I have noticed of late in the press.

===Chinese===
本书以新的形式重版，严格说来并不需要再作一篇序言。不过，既然这里分明是个宜于说个人闲话的地方，我便借这篇作者注的机会，谈谈近来在报刊上注意到的、关于我本人的某些说法所引出的两个问题。

===Original===
One of them bears upon the question of language. I have always felt myself looked upon somewhat in the light of a phenomenon, a position which outside the circus world cannot be regarded as desirable. It needs a special temperament for one to derive much gratification from the fact of being able to do freakish things intentionally, and, as it were, from mere vanity.

===Chinese===
其一关乎语言问题。我一直觉得自己多少被人当作一件奇观来看待——这种处境，在马戏班以外的世界里，实在说不上令人向往。一个人若想从「能够存心做出怪异之事」这一事实中获得多大的满足，仿佛纯然出于虚荣似的，那得有一种特殊的气质才行。

===Original===
The fact of my not writing in my native language has been of course commented upon frequently in reviews and notices of my various works and in the more extended critical articles. I suppose that was unavoidable; and indeed these comments were of the most flattering kind to one’s vanity. But in that matter I have no vanity that could be flattered. I could not have it. The first object of this note is to disclaim any merit there might have been in an act of deliberate volition.

===Chinese===
我不用母语写作这件事，自然屡屡见诸对我各部作品的评论、简讯以及篇幅更长的批评文章。我想这在所难免；而且这些议论若论讨好人虚荣心的程度，实在恭维到了极点。可是在这个问题上，我并没有什么可供恭维的虚荣心。我不可能有。这篇注记的第一个目的，便是要否认那一所谓自觉意志之举可能含有的任何功绩。

===Original===
The impression of my having exercised a choice between the two languages, French and English, both foreign to me, has got abroad somehow. That impression is erroneous. It originated, I believe, in an article written by Sir Hugh Clifford and published in the year ’98, I think, of the last century. Some time before, Sir Hugh Clifford came to see me. He is, if not the first, then one of the first two friends I made for myself by my work, the other being Mr. Cunninghame Graham, who, characteristically enough, had been captivated by my story, “An Outpost of Progress.” These friendships which have endured to this day I count amongst my precious possessions.

===Chinese===
不知怎么，外界竟流传开一种印象，说我曾在法语和英语之间——两者于我都是外语——作过一番选择。这印象是错误的。我相信，它源自休·克利福德爵士写的一篇文章，如果我没记错，发表于上个世纪的 1898 年。在那之前不久，休·克利福德爵士来看过我。他即使算不上我凭自己的作品为自己赢得的第一位朋友，也是头两位朋友之一；另一位是坎宁安·格雷厄姆先生，而最能见出他本色的，是令他倾倒的恰恰是我那篇《进步的前哨》。这几段友谊一直存续至今，我把它们列入自己珍贵的财产。

===Original===
Mr. Hugh Clifford (he was not decorated then) had just published his first volume of Malay sketches. I was naturally delighted to see him and infinitely gratified by the kind things he found to say about my first books and some of my early short stories, the action of which is placed in the Malay Archipelago. I remember that after saying many things which ought to have made me blush to the roots of my hair with outraged modesty, he ended by telling me with the uncompromising yet kindly firmness of a man accustomed to speak unpalatable truths even to Oriental potentates (for their own good of course) that as a matter of fact I didn’t know anything about Malays. I was perfectly aware of this. I have never pretended to any such knowledge, and I was moved﻿—I wonder to this day at my impertinence﻿—to retort: “Of course I don’t know anything about Malays. If I knew only one hundredth part of what you and Frank Swettenham know of Malays I would make everybody sit up.” He went on looking kindly (but firmly) at me and then we both burst out laughing. In the course of that most welcome visit twenty years ago, which I remember so well, we talked of many things; the characteristics of various languages was one of them, and it is on that day that my friend carried away with him the impression that I had exercised a deliberate choice between French and English. Later, when moved by his friendship (no empty word to him) to write a study in the *North American Review* on Joseph Conrad, he conveyed that impression to the public.

===Chinese===
那时休·克利福德先生（他当时还没有获授勋衔）刚出版他的第一本马来亚速写集。我见到他自然高兴，而他对我的头几本书和几篇早期短篇所说的好话——那些故事发生的地点都在马来群岛——更使我无限欣慰。我记得，他把一大堆足以叫我羞得从发根红起、大有损谦逊的好话说完之后，末了用一种既不容分说却又不失和善的坚定口气告诉我——那是一个惯于对东方君主们（自然是为他们好）进逆耳忠言的人才有的口气——说：事实上，我对马来人一无所知。这一点我自己再清楚不过。我从不曾装出诸如此类的学识，而我竟——至今想起自己那时的放肆仍觉诧异——回敬道：「我当然对马来人一无所知。但凡我对马来人的了解，有你和弗兰克·斯威特纳姆的百分之一，我就能叫所有人都坐直了身子。」他继续和善（然而坚定）地望着我，随后我们俩都放声大笑起来。在那次二十年前备受欢迎、至今记忆犹新的拜访中，我们谈了许多事情；各种语言的特性便是话题之一，而正是在那一天，我的朋友随身带走了这样一个印象：我曾在法语与英语之间作过深思熟虑的选择。后来，他出于友谊（这个词于他绝非虚言），在《北美评论》上写了一篇论约瑟夫·康拉德的文章，便把那印象传达给了公众。

===Original===
This misapprehension, for it is nothing else, was no doubt my fault. I must have expressed myself badly in the course of a friendly and intimate talk when one doesn’t watch one’s phrases carefully. My recollection of what I meant to say is: that *had I been under the necessity* of making a choice between the two, and though I knew French fairly well and was familiar with it from infancy, I would have been afraid to attempt expression in a language so perfectly “crystallized.” This, I believe, was the word I used. And then we passed to other matters. I had to tell him a little about myself; and what he told me of his work in the East, his own particular East of which I had but the mistiest, short glimpse, was of the most absorbing interest. The present Governor of Nigeria may not remember that conversation as well as I do, but I am sure that he will not mind this, what in diplomatic language is called “rectification” of a statement made to him by an obscure writer his generous sympathy had prompted him to seek out and make his friend.

===Chinese===
这一误会——它无非是个误会——无疑是因我而起。想必是在那场友好而亲切的谈话中，人说话不字斟句酌的当口，我没把自己的意思表达清楚。我记得我当时想说的是：倘若我当真*必须*在两者之间作一选择，那么，尽管我法语相当好，自幼便熟习它，我也会不敢尝试用一种如此完美地「结晶」了的语言来表情达意。我相信「结晶」正是我当时用的字眼。随后我们便谈起了别的事情。我不得不对他讲了一点我自己的经历；而他对我讲的他在东方的工作——那属于他独有的东方，我只隔着雾气匆匆瞥见过一眼——则引人入胜至极。这位现任尼日利亚总督对那次谈话的记忆也许不及我清楚，但我确信他不会介意这一「更正」——用外交辞令来说——即更正他当年从一位无名作家那里听来的话；正是他慷慨的同情心，当年促使他寻访这位作家并与之结交。

===Original===
The truth of the matter is that my faculty to write in English is as natural as any other aptitude with which I might have been born. I have a strange and overpowering feeling that it had always been an inherent part of myself. English was for me neither a matter of choice nor adoption. The merest idea of choice had never entered my head. And as to adoption﻿—well, yes, there was adoption; but it was I who was adopted by the genius of the language, which directly I came out of the stammering stage made me its own so completely that its very idioms I truly believe had a direct action on my temperament and fashioned my still plastic character.

===Chinese===
事情的真相是：我用英语写作的才能，同我可能生来俱有的任何别的禀赋一样自然。我有一种奇异而不可抗拒的感觉：它从来就是我自身固有的一部分。英语于我，既非选择，亦非收养。选择的念头甚至从未进过我的头脑。至于收养——唔，不错，确有收养这回事；只是被收养的是我：收养我的是这门语言的精灵。我一出结结巴巴的学语阶段，它便把我完全据为己有，以致我真心相信，连它的习语都直接作用于我的气质，塑成了我那尚且柔软可塑的性格。

===Original===
It was a very intimate action and for that very reason it is too mysterious to explain. The task would be as impossible as trying to explain love at first sight. There was something in this conjunction of exulting, almost physical recognition, the same sort of emotional surrender and the same pride of possession, all united in the wonder of a great discovery; but there was on it none of that shadow of dreadful doubt that falls on the very flame of our perishable passions. One knew very well that this was forever.

===Chinese===
那是一种极亲密的作用，也正因其亲密，它才玄妙得无从解释。这桩任务之不可能，恰如解释一见钟情之不可能。在那里面有着某种东西：一种欢欣的、几乎属于肉体相认的辨识，同样的一种情感上的降服，同样的一种据为己有的骄傲，全都汇合在一次重大发现的惊奇之中；然而在那火焰之上，却没有一丝可怕怀疑的暗影——那种暗影，是会落到我们这些终将朽坏的激情的火焰上去的。人心里明白得很：这是永远的了。

===Original===
A matter of discovery and not of inheritance, that very inferiority of the title makes the faculty still more precious, lays the possessor under a lifelong obligation to remain worthy of his great fortune. But it seems to me that all this sounds as if I were trying to explain﻿—a task which I have just pronounced to be impossible. If in action we may admit with awe that the Impossible recedes before men’s indomitable spirit, the Impossible in matters of analysis will always make a stand at some point or other. All I can claim after all those years of devoted practice, with the accumulated anguish of its doubts, imperfections and falterings in my heart, is the right to be believed when I say that if I had not written in English I would not have written at all.

===Chinese===
这是发现，而非继承；恰恰是这名分上的逊色，使这份才能愈加珍贵，使其拥有者背上终生的义务，必须始终无愧于自己的鸿运。但在我听来，这一切仿佛我又在作解释了——而我方才明明断言，解释是不可能的事。在行动的领域，我们尽可以怀着敬畏承认：不可能之事会在人类不屈不挠的精神面前节节后退；可在分析的领域，不可能之事却总会在某一点上站定脚跟，绝不再退。经过那些年全身心的研习践行，心底积攒着其中的疑虑、缺憾与踟蹰之痛，我所能申求的，不过是这样一种权利：当我说「倘若我不是用英语写作，我便根本不会写作」时，人们应当相信我。

===Original===
The other remark which I wish to make here is also a rectification but of a less direct kind. It has nothing to do with the medium of expression. It bears on the matter of my authorship in another way. It is not for me to criticise my judges, the more so because I always felt that I was receiving more than justice at their hands. But it seems to me that their unfailingly interested sympathy has ascribed to racial and historical influences much, of what, I believe, appertains simply to the individual. Nothing is more foreign than what in the literary world is called Sclavonism, to the Polish temperament with its tradition of self-government, its chivalrous view of moral restraints and an exaggerated respect for individual rights: not to mention the important fact that the whole Polish mentality, Western in complexion, had received its training from Italy and France and, historically, had always remained, even in religious matters, in sympathy with the most liberal currents of European thought. An impartial view of humanity in all its degrees of splendour and misery together with a special regard for the rights of the unprivileged of this earth, not on any mystic ground but on the ground of simple fellowship and honourable reciprocity of services, was the dominant characteristic of the mental and moral atmosphere of the houses which sheltered my hazardous childhood:﻿—matters of calm and deep conviction both lasting and consistent, and removed as far as possible from that humanitarianism that seems to be merely a matter of crazy nerves or a morbid conscience.

===Chinese===
我想在这里说的另一番话，也是一种更正，只是不那么直接罢了。它与表达的工具无关，而是从另一个方面关系到我之为作者这件事。评论我的人轮不到我来非议，何况我一向觉得，从他们手里得到的已不止于公道。但在他们那里，那种从不缺席的关切同情，把许多东西归到了种族与历史的影响名下，而那些东西，我相信，本只属于个人。文学界所谓的「斯拉夫主义」，同波兰气质再格格不入不过了——那是一种带着自治传统的气质，对道德约束持侠义的看法，对个人权利怀有过分的敬意：更不必提一个重要的事实：整个波兰心智，底色是西方的，其所受的熏陶来自意大利与法兰西，而且纵观历史，即便在宗教事务上，也始终同情于欧洲思想中最自由的那些潮流。对人类的一切荣枯盛衰作不偏不倚的观照，并且特别顾念这世上无权无势者的权利——不是基于任何神秘的理由，而是基于单纯的伙伴情谊与有来有往的体面道义——这便是荫庇了我那险象环生的童年的那些宅子里，心智与道德氛围的主导特征：——这些都是平静而深沉的信念，既持久又一贯，并且同那种仿佛只是狂乱的神经或病态的良心所作的人道主义，离得越远越好。

===Original===
One of the most sympathetic of my critics tried to account for certain characteristics of my work by the fact of my being, in his own words, “the son of a Revolutionist.” No epithet could be more inapplicable to a man with such a strong sense of responsibility in the region of ideas and action and so indifferent to the promptings of personal ambition as my father. Why the description “revolutionary” should have been applied all through Europe to the Polish risings of 1831 and 1863 I really cannot understand. These risings were purely revolts against foreign domination. The Russians themselves called them “rebellions,” which, from their point of view, was the exact truth. Amongst the men concerned in the preliminaries of the 1863 movement my father was no more revolutionary than the others, in the sense of working for the subversion of any social or political scheme of existence. He was simply a patriot in the sense of a man who believing in the spirituality of a national existence could not bear to see that spirit enslaved.

===Chinese===
在对我最怀好意的批评家中，有一位试图用我——照他自己的说法——是「一个革命者的儿子」这一事实，来解释我作品中的某些特征。对我的父亲那样一个人，再没有比这更不贴切的字眼了：他在思想与行动的领域里责任感何等强烈，对个人野心的怂恿又何等淡漠。「革命的」这一形容何以会通行全欧、加诸 1831 年和 1863 年的波兰起义头上，我实在无法理解。这些起义纯粹是反抗异族统治的起事。俄国人自己就管它们叫「叛乱」——从他们的立场看，这倒是分毫不差的实情。在参与 1863 年运动筹备的人们中间，若说革命是指图谋颠覆某种社会或政治的生活秩序，我的父亲也并不比别人更革命。他只是一个爱国者，在这个意义上：他相信民族的存在自有其精神，而眼见那精神遭受奴役，便不能忍受。

===Original===
Called out publicly in a kindly attempt to justify the work of the son, that figure of my past cannot be dismissed without a few more words. As a child of course I knew very little of my father’s activities, for I was not quite twelve when he died. What I saw with my own eyes was the public funeral, the cleared streets, the hushed crowds; but I understood perfectly well that this was a manifestation of the national spirit seizing a worthy occasion. That bareheaded mass of work people, youths of the University, women at the windows, schoolboys on the pavement, could have known nothing positive about him except the fame of his fidelity to the one guiding emotion in their hearts. I had nothing but that knowledge myself; and this great silent demonstration seemed to me the most natural tribute in the world﻿—not to the man but to the Idea.

===Chinese===
这个往昔的身影，既被人出于好意、为替儿子的作品辩护而公开召了出来，便不好再不多说几句就打发掉。孩提时代，我自然对父亲的活动所知甚少，因为他去世时我还不到十二岁。我亲眼见到的是那场公开的葬礼：清扫一空的街道，噤然无声的人群；但我完全明白，这是民族精神抓住一个相称的时机所作的一次显露。那一片光着头的工人群众、大学里的青年、窗口的妇女、人行道上的学童，对他不可能有任何切实的了解，所知道的唯有那声名：他忠于他们心中唯一的主导情感。我自己所拥有的也无非这一点了解；而这场伟大的无声示威，在我看来是世上再自然不过的敬献——不是敬那个人，而是敬那个观念。

===Original===
What had impressed me much more intimately was the burning of his manuscripts a fortnight or so before his death. It was done under his own superintendence. I happened to go into his room a little earlier than usual that evening, and remaining unnoticed stayed to watch the nursing-sister feeding the blaze in the fireplace. My father sat in a deep armchair propped up with pillows. This is the last time I saw him out of bed. His aspect was to me not so much that of a man desperately ill, as mortally weary﻿—a vanquished man. That act of destruction affected me profoundly by its air of surrender. Not before death however. To a man of such strong faith death could not have been an enemy.

===Chinese===
更深切地印入我心中的，是他去世前约莫两星期焚毁手稿的那件事。那是在他亲自督察下进行的。那天晚上，我碰巧比平常早一点走进他的房间，没被人留意，便留下来看修女护士往壁炉的火里添柴。父亲坐在一把垫着枕头的高背扶手椅里。这是我最后一次看见他离开病榻。他的模样在我眼中，与其说是个病势危殆的人，不如说是个疲惫致死的人——一个被击败的人。那场焚毁，以其投降般的神气深深撼动了我。但那并非向死亡投降。对这样一个信仰坚定的人，死亡不可能成为仇敌。

===Original===
For many years I believed that every scrap of his writings had been burnt, but in July of 1914 the Librarian of the University of Krakow calling on me during our short visit to Poland, mentioned the existence of a few manuscripts of my father and especially of a series of letters written before and during his exile to his most intimate friend who had sent them to the University for preservation. I went to the Library at once, but had only time then for a mere glance. I intended to come back next day and arrange for copies being made of the whole correspondence. But next day there was war. So perhaps I shall never know now what he wrote to his most intimate friend in the time of his domestic happiness, of his new paternity, of his strong hopes﻿—and later, in the hours of disillusion, bereavement and gloom.

===Chinese===
多年来我一直相信，他的文字早已烧得片纸不存；可是 1914 年 7 月，我们短暂访问波兰期间，克拉科夫大学的图书馆馆长来看我，说起尚存有我父亲的几份手稿，尤其是一批他流放之前和流放期间写给至交的信，由那位至交送交大学保存。我当即去了图书馆，却只来得及匆匆一瞥。我本打算第二天再去，安排把全部信件复制下来。可是第二天，战争爆发了。于是，在他享受家庭幸福的日子里、在他初为人父的日子里、在他怀着强烈希望的日子里——以及后来，在幻灭、丧亲与阴郁的时辰里——他给至交写了些什么，我如今也许永远无从知晓了。

===Original===
I had also imagined him to be completely forgotten forty-five years after his death. But this was not the case. Some young men of letters had discovered him, mostly as a remarkable translator of Shakespeare, Victor Hugo and Alfred de Vigny, to whose drama *Chatterton*, translated by himself, he had written an eloquent preface defending the poet’s deep humanity and his ideal of noble stoicism. The political side of his life was being recalled too; for some men of his time, his co-workers in the task of keeping the national spirit firm in the hope of an independent future, had been in their old age publishing their memoirs, where the part he played was for the first time publicly disclosed to the world. I learned then of things in his life I never knew before, things which outside the group of the initiated could have been known to no living being except my mother. It was thus that from a volume of posthumous memoirs dealing with those bitter years I learned the fact that the first inception of the secret National Committee intended primarily to organize moral resistance to the augmented pressure of Russianism arose on my father’s initiative, and that its first meetings were held in our Warsaw house, of which all I remember distinctly is one room, white and crimson, probably the drawing-room. In one of its walls there was the loftiest of all archways. Where it led to remains a mystery; but to this day I cannot get rid of the belief that all this was of enormous proportions, and that the people appearing and disappearing in that immense space were beyond the usual stature of mankind as I got to know it in later life. Amongst them I remember my mother, a more familiar figure than the others, dressed in the black of the national mourning worn in defiance of ferocious police regulations. I have also preserved from that particular time the awe of her mysterious gravity which, indeed, was by no means smileless. For I remember her smiles, too. Perhaps for me she could always find a smile. She was young then, certainly not thirty yet. She died four years later in exile.

===Chinese===
我还曾想象，他死去四十五年后，已被世人遗忘净尽。但事实并非如此。几位年轻文人重新发现了他，主要是把他看作一位不同寻常的翻译家，译过莎士比亚、维克多·雨果和阿尔弗雷德·德·维尼；他还为自己所译的维尼剧作《夏特顿》写过一篇雄辩的序言，为那位诗人的深厚人性与他那高贵的斯多葛式理想辩护。他生平的政治一面也正被人重新忆起；因为他那个时代的一些人——那些曾同他协力、在独立未来的希望中使民族精神保持坚定的人们——到了晚年纷纷刊行回忆录，他所扮演的角色这才第一次公之于世。我由此得知了他生平中一些我从来不曾知晓的事情；这些事情，在知情者的小圈子之外，除了我的母亲，不可能为任何一个在世之人所知。就是这样，从一部追述那些惨苦年月的身后回忆录里，我得知了这样一件事实：那个最初旨在组织道义抵抗、以对抗俄罗斯主义变本加厉的压迫的秘密国家委员会，最初是在我父亲的倡议下发起的，它最早的几次会议，便在我们华沙的宅子里举行。那宅子我至今清楚记得的，只有一个房间，白色与绯红色的，多半是客厅。房间的一面墙上有一道拱门，比一切拱门都高。它通向何处，至今是个谜；但直到今天，我仍摆脱不掉这样一个信念：那一切规模浩大之至，在那巨大空间里时隐时现的人们，都超乎我日后生活中所认识的人类的一般身材。在他们中间，我记得我的母亲，一个比别人更熟稔的身影，穿着民族志哀的黑衣——那是公然违抗凶暴的警察条规而穿的。从那个特殊的时期，我还保存着对她那神秘庄重的敬畏；而那份庄重，其实绝没有到不苟言笑的地步。因为我也记得她的微笑。也许对我，她总能寻得出一丝微笑。她那时还年轻，肯定不满三十岁。四年后，她死在了流放之地。

===Original===
In the pages which follow I mention her visit to her brother’s house about a year before her death. I also speak a little of my father as I remember him in the years following what was for him the deadly blow of her loss. And now, having been again evoked in answer to the words of a friendly critic, these Shades may be allowed to return to their place of rest where their forms in life linger yet, dim but poignant, and awaiting the moment when their haunting reality, their last trace on earth, shall pass forever with me out of the world.

===Chinese===
在以下的篇幅里，我提到过她在去世前约一年到她兄弟家中作客的事。我也稍稍谈了我的父亲，谈他在遭受丧妻这一致命打击之后的岁月里、留在我记忆中的样子。而现在，这些幽影既已被一位友善的批评家的话语再度唤起，也就可以准许他们回到安息之地去了——在那里，他们生前的形影犹自徘徊，黯淡却锥心，等待着那萦回不去的真实、他们在人世间的最后痕迹，随我一同永远离开这个世界的时刻。

===Original===
J. C.

===Chinese===
J. C.

===Original===
1919.

===Chinese===
1919.

## A Familiar Preface / 闲话序言

===Original===
As a general rule we do not want much encouragement to talk about ourselves; yet this little book is the result of a friendly suggestion, and even of a little friendly pressure. I defended myself with some spirit; but, with characteristic tenacity, the friendly voice insisted, “You know, you really must.”

===Chinese===
按常例，要我们谈论自己，是用不着多少怂恿的；然而这本小书，却是一位友人出言相劝、甚至略施友谊压力的结果。我也曾振作精神为自己辩解了一番；但那友好的声音凭着它特有的执拗，一味坚持：「你要知道，你实在非写不可。」

===Original===
It was not an argument, but I submitted at once. If one must!﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
这算不上什么论证，我却立刻屈服了。既然非做不可！……

===Original===
You perceive the force of a word. He who wants to persuade should put his trust not in the right argument, but in the right word. The power of sound has always been greater than the power of sense. I don’t say this by way of disparagement. It is better for mankind to be impressionable than reflective. Nothing humanely great﻿—great, I mean, as affecting a whole mass of lives﻿—has come from reflection. On the other hand, you cannot fail to see the power of mere words; such words as Glory, for instance, or Pity. I won’t mention any more. They are not far to seek. Shouted with perseverance, with ardour, with conviction, these two by their sound alone have set whole nations in motion and upheaved the dry, hard ground on which rests our whole social fabric. There’s “virtue” for you if you like!﻿ ﻿… Of course the accent must be attended to. The right accent. That’s very important. The capacious lung, the thundering or the tender vocal chords. Don’t talk to me of your Archimedes’ lever. He was an absentminded person with a mathematical imagination. Mathematics commands all my respect, but I have no use for engines. Give me the right word and the right accent and I will move the world.

===Chinese===
你这就瞧得出一个字眼的力量了。想要说服人的人，不该信赖正确的论证，而该信赖正确的字眼。声音的力量，向来大于意义的力量。我说这话并非贬损。人类易受感动，总比长于思索要好。人间凡属伟大的东西——所谓伟大，我是指能影响一大片人生的东西——没有一样是出自思索的。另一方面，光凭字眼的力量，你也不可能看不见；比如「光荣」这样的字眼，或者「怜悯」。我不再多举了。它们并不难找。这两个词，只要喊得坚持不懈、喊得热烈、喊得深信不疑，单凭其声音，便曾驱动整个整个的民族，掀翻了我们整个社会结构所依托的那片干硬的土地。你若愿意，这就叫「德行」！……当然，重音还得讲究。正确的重音。这非常重要。洪阔的肺活量，那或如雷吼、或似轻抚的声带。别跟我提你那阿基米德的杠杆。他是个心不在焉、满脑子数学想象的人。数学令我肃然起敬，但我用不着机械。给我正确的字眼和正确的重音，我就能移动世界。

===Original===
What a dream for a writer! Because written words have their accent, too. Yes! Let me only find the right word! Surely it must be lying somewhere among the wreckage of all the plaints and all the exultations poured out aloud since the first day when hope, the undying, came down on earth. It may be there, close by, disregarded, invisible, quite at hand. But it’s no good. I believe there are men who can lay hold of a needle in a pottle of hay at the first try. For myself, I have never had such luck.

===Chinese===
这对一个作家是多好的梦！因为写下的字眼，也有它们的重音。是啊！但愿我能找到那个正确的字眼！它必定躺在什么地方——躺在自希望——那不死的希望——降临世上的第一天起，人类大声倾吐的一切哀叹与一切欢腾的残骸之间。它也许就在近旁，无人理会，无形无影，近在咫尺。可是没有用。我相信世上有人能一伸手就从一捆干草里拈出针来。至于我自己，从来不曾有过这样的运气。

===Original===
And then there is that accent. Another difficulty. For who is going to tell whether the accent is right or wrong till the word is shouted, and fails to be heard, perhaps, and goes downwind, leaving the world unmoved? Once upon a time there lived an emperor who was a sage and something of a literary man. He jotted down on ivory tablets thoughts, maxims, reflections which chance has preserved for the edification of posterity. Among other sayings﻿—I am quoting from memory﻿—I remember this solemn admonition: “Let all thy words have the accent of heroic truth.” The accent of heroic truth! This is very fine, but I am thinking that it is an easy matter for an austere emperor to jot down grandiose advice. Most of the working truths on this earth are humble, not heroic; and there have been times in the history of mankind when the accents of heroic truth have moved it to nothing but derision.

===Chinese===
再说还有那重音。又一层困难。因为字眼还没喊出口，谁能断定重音是对是错？要等喊出去之后，也许没人听见，随风而逝，世界依然不为所动。从前有一位皇帝，他是位哲人，也算得半个文人。他在象牙板上记下一些思想、格言、感想，机缘凑巧把它们保存下来，以启迪后人。在他的那些话里——我这里是凭记忆引用——我记得有这么一句庄严的训诫：「愿你的一切言语，都带着英雄的真实的重音。」英雄的真实的重音！这话漂亮得很；不过我想，一位严峻的皇帝随手写下冠冕堂皇的箴言，原是容易的事。这世上实际起作用的真理，大多是谦卑的，不是英雄的；而且在人类历史上，曾有过那样的时代，「英雄的真实」的重音只换来世人的讪笑。

===Original===
Nobody will expect to find between the covers of this little book words of extraordinary potency or accents of irresistible heroism. However humiliating for my self-esteem, I must confess that the counsels of Marcus Aurelius are not for me. They are more fit for a moralist than for an artist. Truth of a modest sort I can promise you, and also sincerity. That complete, praiseworthy sincerity which, while it delivers one into the hands of one’s enemies, is as likely as not to embroil one with one’s friends.

===Chinese===
谁也不会指望在这本小书的封皮之间，找到效力非凡的字眼，或不可抗拒的英雄腔调。尽管有伤我的自尊，我也必须承认：马可·奥勒留的教诲于我无缘。它们更宜于道德家，而不宜于艺术家。谦卑一路的真实，我倒可以应许你们，还有真诚。那种十全十美、值得称道的真诚——它一面把你交到仇人手里，一面十有八九又要让你同朋友们闹翻。

===Original===
“Embroil” is perhaps too strong an expression. I can’t imagine among either my enemies or my friends a being so hard up for something to do as to quarrel with me. “To disappoint one’s friends” would be nearer the mark. Most, almost all, friendships of the writing period of my life have come to me through my books; and I know that a novelist lives in his work. He stands there, the only reality in an invented world, among imaginary things, happenings, and people. Writing about them, he is only writing about himself. But the disclosure is not complete. He remains, to a certain extent, a figure behind the veil; a suspected rather than a seen presence﻿—a movement and a voice behind the draperies of fiction. In these personal notes there is no such veil. And I cannot help thinking of a passage in the *Imitation of Christ* where the ascetic author, who knew life so profoundly, says that “there are persons esteemed on their reputation who by showing themselves destroy the opinion one had of them.” This is the danger incurred by an author of fiction who sets out to talk about himself without disguise.

===Chinese===
「闹翻」这话也许重了些。我想象不出，在我的仇人里或朋友里，会有谁闲到非找件事做不可、来同我吵架的地步。「让朋友们失望」倒更近乎实情。我人生中写作时期的友谊，几乎全部是经由我的书来到我身边的；而我知道，小说家就活在他的作品里。他站在那里，是一个虚构世界里的唯一实在，置身于想象的事物、事件和人物之间。写它们，他只是在写他自己。但这袒露并不彻底。在某种程度上，他依然是个帷幕后的人物；是一个被猜疑而未被看见的存在——小说的帷幕后面的一下动静、一个声音。在这些个人札记里，就没有这样的帷幕了。而我不由得想起《效法基督》里的一段话，那位对人生知之极深的苦修作者说道：「有些人负着令名，一朝露面，便毁掉了人们原先对他们的看法。」这正是一个决意不加掩饰地谈论自己的小说作者所要担的风险。

===Original===
While these reminiscent pages were appearing serially I was remonstrated with for bad economy; as if such writing were a form of self-indulgence wasting the substance of future volumes. It seems that I am not sufficiently literary. Indeed, a man who never wrote a line for print till he was thirty-six cannot bring himself to look upon his existence and his experience, upon the sum of his thoughts, sensations, and emotions, upon his memories and his regrets, and the whole possession of his past, as only so much material for his hands. Once before, some three years ago, when I published *The Mirror of the Sea*, a volume of impressions and memories, the same remarks were made to me. Practical remarks. But, truth to say, I have never understood the kind of thrift they recommend. I wanted to pay my tribute to the sea, its ships and its men, to whom I remain indebted for so much which has gone to make me what I am. That seemed to me the only shape in which I could offer it to their shades. There could not be a question in my mind of anything else. It is quite possible that I am a bad economist; but it is certain that I am incorrigible.

===Chinese===
这些回忆篇章分期连载的时候，曾有人责备我不善于精打细算；仿佛这样的写作是一种自我放纵，浪费了未来几卷书的材料似的。看来我不够文学。的确，一个到三十六岁才头一回为印刷写下一行字的人，没法让自己把生存与经历、把思想、感受与情绪的总和、把记忆与追悔、把过去的全部所有，仅仅看作自己手头的材料。大约三年前，我出版《大海如镜》——一部印象与回忆的结集——的时候，同样的话也有人对我讲过。很实际的话。可是说实在的，我从来就不懂他们所推荐的那种节俭。我想要向大海、向它的船和它的船员致敬——我之所以成为今日之我，欠它们的实在太多。在我看来，那是我唯一能把这份敬意献给它们的幽影的形式。我心里容不下任何别的问题。我很可能是个不会算计的人；但无可置疑，我已不可救药。

===Original===
Having matured in the surroundings and under the special conditions of sea life, I have a special piety toward that form of my past; for its impressions were vivid, its appeal direct, its demands such as could be responded to with the natural elation of youth and strength equal to the call. There was nothing in them to perplex a young conscience. Having broken away from my origins under a storm of blame from every quarter which had the merest shadow of right to voice an opinion, removed by great distances from such natural affections as were still left to me, and even estranged, in a measure, from them by the totally unintelligible character of the life which had seduced me so mysteriously from my allegiance, I may safely say that through the blind force of circumstances the sea was to be all my world and the merchant service my only home for a long succession of years. No wonder, then, that in my two exclusively sea books﻿—*The Nigger of the Narcissus*, and *The Mirror of the Sea* (and in the few short sea stories like “Youth” and “Typhoon”)﻿—I have tried with an almost filial regard to render the vibration of life in the great world of waters, in the hearts of the simple men who have for ages traversed its solitudes, and also that something sentient which seems to dwell in ships﻿—the creatures of their hands and the objects of their care.

===Chinese===
我既是在海上生活的环境里、在它特殊的条件下成熟的，便对自己过去的这一段怀有一种特殊的虔敬；因为那段生活印象鲜明，召唤直接，它的要求又无不可以拿青春的昂扬与当得起这召唤的膂力去回应。其中没有任何叫年轻良心为难的东西。想当年，我顶着一场责备的风暴脱离了我的出身之地——但凡还有一丝一毫权利发表意见的四方，无不对我责备——又被迢迢距离隔开了我所剩不多的天然亲情，甚至在一定程度上，由于那种如此神秘地诱我背离本分的生活全然无从索解，连这点亲情也和我疏远了。我可以有把握地说：凭着情势的盲力，多年来大海注定就是我整个的世界，商船队就是我唯一的家。无怪乎，在我那两本纯然写海的书——《「水仙号」上的黑水手》与《大海如镜》（以及《青春》《台风》那几篇海上短篇）——里，我怀着近乎孝意的关注，试图传达那浩瀚水世界里生命的悸动，传达那些世世代代跋涉于其荒寂之中的朴实人的心，也传达那仿佛栖居于船舶之中的某种有灵之物——船舶是他们双手造出来的，也是他们操心照拂的对象。

===Original===
One’s literary life must turn frequently for sustenance to memories and seek discourse with the shades, unless one has made up one’s mind to write only in order to reprove mankind for what it is, or praise it for what it is not, or﻿—generally﻿—to teach it how to behave. Being neither quarrelsome, nor a flatterer, nor a sage, I have done none of these things, and I am prepared to put up serenely with the insignificance which attaches to persons who are not meddlesome in some way or other. But resignation is not indifference. I would not like to be left standing as a mere spectator on the bank of the great stream carrying onward so many lives. I would fain claim for myself the faculty of so much insight as can be expressed in a voice of sympathy and compassion.

===Chinese===
一个人的文学生活，必须常常向记忆寻求滋养，与幽影交谈，除非他已打定主意，写作只是为了责备人类的现状，或称颂它并未拥有的东西，再不然——总而言之——是为了教训人类如何做人。我既不好争，又不谄媚，也不是哲人，这些事一件也没有做，所以我准备安然承受那份无足轻重——凡是不肯以这样那样的方式多事的人，都要沾上这份无足轻重。但是听天由命不等于冷漠。我不愿意只是站在岸上，做一个旁观者，眼看那条载着这么多生命前行的大河滔滔流去。我倒愿意为自己要求这样一种洞察力——其分量恰可以借一种同情与怜悯的声音表达出来的洞察力。

===Original===
It seems to me that in one, at least, authoritative quarter of criticism I am suspected of a certain unemotional, grim acceptance of facts﻿—of what the French would call *sécheresse du coeur*. Fifteen years of unbroken silence before praise or blame testify sufficiently to my respect for criticism, that fine flower of personal expression in the garden of letters. But this is more of a personal matter, reaching the man behind the work, and therefore it may be alluded to in a volume which is a personal note in the margin of the public page. Not that I feel hurt in the least. The charge﻿—if it amounted to a charge at all﻿—was made in the most considerate terms; in a tone of regret.

===Chinese===
依我看，至少在批评界的一个权威角落里，我被人疑心有一种不动感情的、冷硬的事实接受态度——也就是法国人所谓的 *sécheresse du coeur*（心灵的枯燥）。十五年来，面对毁誉我一律缄默，这足以证明我对批评的敬意——批评，那是文学花园里个人表达的一朵好花。但这一桩更是个人的事，触及作品背后的那个人，因此不妨在一个这样的集子里提上一句——这个集子，本就是公共书页边上的一条个人小注。倒不是我感到丝毫委屈。这一指摘——如果也算得上指摘的话——措辞极为体谅，用的是惋惜的口吻。

===Original===
My answer is that if it be true that every novel contains an element of autobiography﻿—and this can hardly be denied, since the creator can only express himself in his creation﻿—then there are some of us to whom an open display of sentiment is repugnant. I would not unduly praise the virtue of restraint. It is often merely temperamental. But it is not always a sign of coldness. It may be pride. There can be nothing more humiliating than to see the shaft of one’s emotion miss the mark of either laughter or tears. Nothing more humiliating! And this for the reason that should the mark be missed, should the open display of emotion fail to move, then it must perish unavoidably in disgust or contempt. No artist can be reproached for shrinking from a risk which only fools run to meet and only genius dare confront with impunity. In a task which mainly consists in laying one’s soul more or less bare to the world, a regard for decency, even at the cost of success, is but the regard for one’s own dignity which is inseparably united with the dignity of one’s work.

===Chinese===
我的回答是：如果每一部小说都含有自传的成分——这一点几乎无可否认，因为创造者只能在他的创造物中表现自己——那么我们中间有些人，对感情的公然流露是深恶痛绝的。我不愿过分称颂克制之德。那往往只是气质使然。但它未必总是冷漠的标志。它也可能是自尊。眼见自己感情的箭失了靶——既没射中笑，也没射中泪——再没有比这更丢人的了。再没有更丢人的了！而原因在于：一旦失靶，一旦感情的公然流露未能动人，它便不可避免地要在厌恶或轻蔑中毁灭。有一种风险，只有蠢人才奔着去迎，只有天才才敢于无所顾忌地面对——艺术家规避这种风险，是谁也责备不得的。在一件主要在于把自己的灵魂或多或少袒露给世人的工作里，顾及体面——哪怕以成功为代价——无非是顾及自己的尊严，而这尊严同自己作品的尊严，是不可分地连在一起的。

===Original===
And then﻿—it is very difficult to be wholly joyous or wholly sad on this earth. The comic, when it is human, soon takes upon itself a face of pain; and some of our griefs (some only, not all, for it is the capacity for suffering which makes man august in the eyes of men) have their source in weaknesses which must be recognized with smiling compassion as the common inheritance of us all. Joy and sorrow in this world pass into each other, mingling their forms and their murmurs in the twilight of life as mysterious as an overshadowed ocean, while the dazzling brightness of supreme hopes lies far off, fascinating and still, on the distant edge of the horizon.

===Chinese===
再说——在这世上，要全然欢喜或全然悲哀，是很难的。喜剧性的东西一旦关乎人事，很快便带上痛苦的面容；而我们的某些悲伤（只是某些，并非全部，因为唯有承受痛苦的能力，才使人在人的眼中显得庄严）源自种种弱点，必须带着含笑的怜悯认下它们，当作我们共有的遗产。这个世上的欢乐与悲哀彼此转化，在人生的暮色里混融着各自的形影与低语——那暮色神秘如一片阴影笼罩的大海，而最高希望的耀眼光华，则远远躺在天际的边缘，迷人而静止。

===Original===
Yes! I, too, would like to hold the magic wand giving that command over laughter and tears which is declared to be the highest achievement of imaginative literature. Only, to be a great magician one must surrender oneself to occult and irresponsible powers, either outside or within one’s breast. We have all heard of simple men selling their souls for love or power to some grotesque devil. The most ordinary intelligence can perceive without much reflection that anything of the sort is bound to be a fool’s bargain. I don’t lay claim to particular wisdom because of my dislike and distrust of such transactions. It may be my sea training acting upon a natural disposition to keep good hold on the one thing really mine, but the fact is that I have a positive horror of losing even for one moving moment that full possession of myself which is the first condition of good service. And I have carried my notion of good service from my earlier into my later existence. I, who have never sought in the written word anything else but a form of the Beautiful﻿—I have carried over that article of creed from the decks of ships to the more circumscribed space of my desk, and by that act, I suppose, I have become permanently imperfect in the eyes of the ineffable company of pure esthetes.

===Chinese===
是啊！我也想握住那根魔杖，获得对笑与泪的支配——据说那是想象文学的最高成就。只是，要做大魔术师，人就得把自己交给种种玄秘而不负责任的力量，不论这力量在胸内还是胸外。我们都听说过一些头脑简单的人，为了爱情或权势，把自己的灵魂卖给某个怪模怪样的魔鬼。最寻常的智力也不难看出，这等买卖注定是桩蠢人的交易。我并不因为厌恶、不信任这类交易，就自命有何特别的智慧。这也许是我海上训练的功夫，作用于一种要把真正属于自己的那件东西牢牢握住的天性；但事实是，哪怕只在动情的一瞬失去对自我的完全占有——那是良好服务的首要条件——我都怀有一种实实在在的恐怖。而我已把良好服务的观念，从先前的生涯带进了后来的生涯。我这个人，在文字里从来不曾寻求过任何别的东西，只寻求「美」的一种形式——我把这一条信条，从船舶的甲板带进了书桌前这方较为狭窄的天地；而由于这一举动，我想，在那班高妙得难以言喻的纯粹审美家眼中，我已经永远地不完美了。

===Original===
As in political so in literary action a man wins friends for himself mostly by the passion of his prejudices and by the consistent narrowness of his outlook. But I have never been able to love what was not lovable or hate what was not hateful out of deference for some general principle. Whether there be any courage in making this admission I know not. After the middle turn of life’s way we consider dangers and joys with a tranquil mind. So I proceed in peace to declare that I have always suspected in the effort to bring into play the extremities of emotions the debasing touch of insincerity. In order to move others deeply we must deliberately allow ourselves to be carried away beyond the bounds of our normal sensibility﻿—innocently enough, perhaps, and of necessity, like an actor who raises his voice on the stage above the pitch of natural conversation﻿—but still we have to do that. And surely this is no great sin. But the danger lies in the writer becoming the victim of his own exaggeration, losing the exact notion of sincerity, and in the end coming to despise truth itself as something too cold, too blunt for his purpose﻿—as, in fact, not good enough for his insistent emotion. From laughter and tears the descent is easy to snivelling and giggles.

===Chinese===
一如在政治行动中，在文学行动中，一个人为自己赢得朋友，多半靠的是他偏见的激情，和他一以贯之的狭隘眼界。可是我从来做不到——为了尊奉某条笼统的原则——去爱那不可爱的，或恨那不可恨的。作这番承认究竟有没有勇气，我不知道。人生之路转过中段的弯以后，我们看待危险与欢乐，心境都已平静。所以我安然申明：我一向疑心，在把情感推向极端的努力里，总带着一丝不真诚的堕落气息。要深深打动别人，我们就得存心任凭自己被带出正常感受的疆界——也许够无辜，也属必要，就像演员在舞台上把嗓门抬高到日常谈话的音调之上——可我们总归得这么做。这当然算不上大罪。但危险在于：作家成了自己夸张的牺牲品，失去了对真诚的精确意识，末了竟轻蔑起真实本身，嫌它对他的用途来说太冷、太直——事实上，是配不上他那执拗的情感。从笑与泪滑到抽抽搭搭与嘻嘻哈哈，下坡路是很容易的。

===Original===
These may seem selfish considerations; but you can’t, in sound morals, condemn a man for taking care of his own integrity. It is his clear duty. And least of all can you condemn an artist pursuing, however humbly and imperfectly, a creative aim. In that interior world where his thought and his emotions go seeking for the experience of imagined adventures, there are no policemen, no law, no pressure of circumstance or dread of opinion to keep him within bounds. Who then is going to say Nay to his temptations if not his conscience?

===Chinese===
这些考虑也许显得自私；但按照健全的道德，你不能责备一个人看护自己的操守。那分明是他的义务。而尤其不能责备的，是一个无论多么卑微、多么不完满地追求着创造目标的艺术家。在他那个内心世界里——他的思想与情感在其中寻访想象奇遇的经验——没有警察，没有法律，没有情势的压力，也没有对舆论的畏惧来约束他。那么，还有谁来对他的诱惑说「不」呢，如果不是他的良心？

===Original===
And besides﻿—this, remember, is the place and the moment of perfectly open talk﻿—I think that all ambitions are lawful except those which climb upward on the miseries or credulities of mankind. All intellectual and artistic ambitions are permissible, up to and even beyond the limit of prudent sanity. They can hurt no one. If they are mad, then so much the worse for the artist. Indeed, as virtue is said to be, such ambitions are their own reward. Is it such a very mad presumption to believe in the sovereign power of one’s art, to try for other means, for other ways of affirming this belief in the deeper appeal of one’s work? To try to go deeper is not to be insensible. An historian of hearts is not an historian of emotions, yet he penetrates further, restrained as he may be, since his aim is to reach the very fount of laughter and tears. The sight of human affairs deserves admiration and pity. They are worthy of respect, too. And he is not insensible who pays them the undemonstrative tribute of a sigh which is not a sob, and of a smile which is not a grin. Resignation, not mystic, not detached, but resignation open-eyed, conscious, and informed by love, is the only one of our feelings for which it is impossible to become a sham.

===Chinese===
再说——请记住，此地此时是开诚布公的场合——我认为一切抱负都是正当的，除了那种踩着人类的苦难或轻信往上爬的。一切智力的、艺术的抱负都是可容许的，直到甚至超出审慎神志的界限。它们伤害不了任何人。如果它们是疯癫，那也只能由艺术家自己去承受更坏的后果。真的，正如人们说美德自是其报偿一样，这类抱负本身就是它们的报酬。相信自己的艺术有至高之力，尝试别的手段、别的途径，来印证自己这份信念——相信自己的作品能打动更深的地方——这难道是很疯癫的僭妄吗？力求深入，并不就是麻木不仁。心灵的史家不是情绪的史家，但尽管有所克制，他反而穿透得更深，因为他的目标正是抵达笑与泪的源头。人间万事的景象，值得钦佩，也值得怜悯。它们也值得尊重。而一个人向它们致以不事张扬的敬礼——一声不是呜咽的叹息，一个不是龇牙咧嘴的微笑——他绝不是麻木不仁。听天由命——不是神秘的，不是超然的，而是睁着眼的、清醒的、由爱灌注的听天由命——是我们的种种情感中唯一不可能变成赝品的一种。

===Original===
Not that I think resignation the last word of wisdom. I am too much the creature of my time for that. But I think that the proper wisdom is to will what the gods will without, perhaps, being certain what their will is﻿—or even if they have a will of their own. And in this matter of life and art it is not the Why that matters so much to our happiness as the How. As the Frenchman said, “*Il y a toujours la manière.*” Very true. Yes. There is the manner. The manner in laughter, in tears, in irony, in indignations and enthusiasms, in judgments﻿—and even in love. The manner in which, as in the features and character of a human face, the inner truth is foreshadowed for those who know how to look at their kind.

===Chinese===
倒不是我认为听天由命便是智慧的最后一句。要做那样想，我未免太是我这个时代的产儿了。但我以为恰当的智慧是：愿神之所愿，却也许并不确知神的意愿是什么——甚至不确知神究竟有没有自己的意愿。而在人生与艺术这件事上，关乎我们幸福的，与其说是「为何」，不如说是「如何」。正如那位法国人说的：「*Il y a toujours la manière.*」（手法总是有的。）很对。是的。就有这个手法。笑时的手法，流泪时的手法，讥诮时的手法，愤慨与热衷时的手法，下判断时的手法——甚至恋爱时的手法。凭借这手法，一如凭借一张人脸的容貌与性情，内在的真实便为那些懂得如何看待同类的人预示出来。

===Original===
Those who read me know my conviction that the world, the temporal world, rests on a few very simple ideas; so simple that they must be as old as the hills. It rests notably, among others, on the idea of Fidelity. At a time when nothing which is not revolutionary in some way or other can expect to attract much attention I have not been revolutionary in my writings. The revolutionary spirit is mighty convenient in this, that it frees one from all scruples as regards ideas. Its hard, absolute optimism is repulsive to my mind by the menace of fanaticism and intolerance it contains. No doubt one should smile at these things; but, imperfect Esthete, I am no better Philosopher. All claim to special righteousness awakens in me that scorn and anger from which a philosophical mind should be free.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
读过我的人都知道我的一个信念：这个世界，这尘世的世界，立在几个极简单的观念上；简单得必定与山丘一样古老。它尤其立在其一之上，那就是忠贞的观念。在这样一个时代——凡不以某种方式带点革命气的东西，都休指望引人瞩目——我在自己的写作里却并不革命。革命精神有一大便利：它解除了人在观念问题上的一切顾忌。它那种生硬的、绝对的乐观主义，因其内含的狂热与不宽容的威胁，为我所反感。毫无疑问，对这类事情本该一笑置之；然而，身为不完满的审美家，我也并不更高明，做不成哲人。凡自称独占正义的主张，都会在我心中唤起那种鄙夷与愤怒——哲人的心本该免于此。……

===Original===
I fear that trying to be conversational I have only managed to be unduly discursive. I have never been very well acquainted with the art of conversation﻿—that art which, I understand, is supposed to be lost now. My young days, the days when one’s habits and character are formed, have been rather familiar with long silences. Such voices as broke into them were anything but conversational. No. I haven’t got the habit. Yet this discursiveness is not so irrelevant to the handful of pages which follow. They, too, have been charged with discursiveness, with disregard of chronological order (which is in itself a crime), with unconventionality of form (which is an impropriety). I was told severely that the public would view with displeasure the informal character of my recollections. “Alas!” I protested, mildly. “Could I begin with the sacramental words, ‘I was born on such a date in such a place?’ The remoteness of the locality would have robbed the statement of all interest. I haven’t lived through wonderful adventures to be related seriatim. I haven’t known distinguished men on whom I could pass fatuous remarks. I haven’t been mixed up with great or scandalous affairs. This is but a bit of psychological document, and even so, I haven’t written it with a view to put forward any conclusion of my own.”

===Chinese===
我担心，想求一个娓娓而谈，结果只落得过分枝蔓。我向来不大熟谙谈话的艺术——据我所知，这门艺术如今是失传了。我的年轻时代，那习惯与性格正在成形的年代，倒是与长长的沉默颇为相熟。偶或打破沉默的声音，又绝非谈话之流。不。我没有这习惯。然而这点枝蔓，与随后的寥寥几页也并非全无关系。那几页也被指为枝蔓、指为无视编年顺序（这本身就是一桩罪过）、指为形式不合常规（这是一桩失体）。有人严厉地对我说，我这些回忆录不拘形迹的性质，将招致公众的不快。「唉！」我温和地抗议道，「难道我能用那句神圣不可易的开场白——『我于某年某月某日生于某地』——来起头么？那地方之遥远，早把这声明的一切兴味都剥夺得干干净净。我没有经历过什么可以按部就班一一铺叙的了不起的奇遇。我不曾结识什么显赫人物，可以对他们发一通昏聩的评语。我没有卷入过什么重大的或臭名昭著的事件。这不过是一份心理学的文献片段；即便是文献，我写它也不是为了提出任何我自己的结论。」

===Original===
But my objector was not placated. These were good reasons for not writing at all﻿—not a defence of what stood written already, he said.

===Chinese===
可我的反对者并未就此平息。他说，这些都是不写作的好理由——却不是对已然写成之物的辩护。

===Original===
I admit that almost anything, anything in the world, would serve as a good reason for not writing at all. But since I have written them, all I want to say in their defence is that these memories put down without any regard for established conventions have not been thrown off without system and purpose. They have their hope and their aim. The hope that from the reading of these pages there may emerge at last the vision of a personality; the man behind the books so fundamentally dissimilar as, for instance, *Almayer’s Folly* and *The Secret Agent*, and yet a coherent, justifiable personality both in its origin and in its action. This is the hope. The immediate aim, closely associated with the hope, is to give the record of personal memories by presenting faithfully the feelings and sensations connected with the writing of my first book and with my first contact with the sea.

===Chinese===
我承认，几乎任何东西，世上任何东西，都可以充当根本不写作的好理由。可是既然已经写了，我在辩护时想说的只是：这些全然不顾成规而记下的回忆，并不是没有章法、没有目的地随手抛出的。它们有它们的希望，也有它们的目标。希望是：从这几页文字的阅读中，最终或许能显现出一个人的形象——那个藏在书背后的人；那些书彼此根本不相类似，比如《阿尔迈耶的愚蠢》与《间谍》，然而他是一个连贯的、说得通的人，无论在其来历还是在其行事上。这就是希望。而与之紧密相连的直接目标是：通过忠实地呈现与我写作第一本书、与我初识大海相连的感受与感觉，给出一份个人记忆的记录。

===Original===
In the purposely mingled resonance of this double strain a friend here and there will perhaps detect a subtle accord.

===Chinese===
在这两重音调有意为之的交鸣里，散在各处的一位友人，或许会听出一缕微妙的和声。

===Original===
J. C.

===Chinese===
J. C.

## I / I

===Original===
Books may be written in all sorts of places. Verbal inspiration may enter the berth of a mariner on board a ship frozen fast in a river in the middle of a town; and since saints are supposed to look benignantly on humble believers, I indulge in the pleasant fancy that the shade of old Flaubert﻿—who imagined himself to be (amongst other things) a descendant of Vikings﻿—might have hovered with amused interest over the docks of a 2,000-ton steamer called the *Adowa*, on board of which, gripped by the inclement winter alongside a quay in Rouen, the tenth chapter of *Almayer’s Folly* was begun. With interest, I say, for was not the kind Norman giant with enormous moustaches and a thundering voice the last of the Romantics? Was he not, in his unworldly, almost ascetic, devotion to his art a sort of literary, saintlike hermit?

===Chinese===
书可以在五花八门的地方写成。字句的灵感，未尝不能进入一条冻牢在城镇中央河道里的船上、一个水手的铺位；既然想必圣者肯垂青卑微的信徒，我便耽于这样一个愉快的幻想：老福楼拜——他自命（在诸多身份之外）是维京人的后裔——的幽魂，或许曾饶有兴味地盘旋在一艘名叫「阿多瓦号」的 2,000 吨汽轮的船坞上空；正是在那条船上、在鲁昂一处码头旁被严冬钳住的时候，《阿尔迈耶的愚蠢》的第十章起了笔。我说饶有兴味，因为这位蓄着大胡子、嗓音如雷的善良诺曼巨人，难道不是最后一位浪漫主义者吗？以他不慕荣利、近乎苦修的艺术献身而论，他难道不正是文学的一位圣者般的隐士吗？

===Original===
“ ‘It has set at last,’ said Nina to her mother, pointing to the hills behind which the sun had sunk.”﻿ ﻿… These words of Almayer’s romantic daughter I remember tracing on the grey paper of a pad which rested on the blanket of my bed-place. They referred to a sunset in Malayan Isles and shaped themselves in my mind, in a hallucinated vision of forests and rivers and seas, far removed from a commercial and yet romantic town of the northern hemisphere. But at that moment the mood of visions and words was cut short by the third officer, a cheerful and casual youth, coming in with a bang of the door and the exclamation: “You’ve made it jolly warm in here.”

===Chinese===
「『它终于落下去了，』妮娜对母亲说，手指着群山，太阳就沉在那山后。」……阿尔迈耶这位浪漫女儿的这几句话，我记得是写在一叠灰色稿纸上的；稿纸摊在我铺位的毯子上。它们说的是马来群岛上的一次日落，却在我的头脑里、在一片森林、河流与大海的幻视之中成了形——远在一个北半球的商业而同时浪漫的城市之外。可就在那一刻，幻象与文字的心绪被三副打断了：一个快快活活、随随便便的年轻人，砰地一声推门进来，嚷道：「你这儿可弄得真暖和。」

===Original===
It was warm. I had turned on the steam-heater after placing a tin under the leaky water-cock﻿—for perhaps you do not know that water will leak where steam will not. I am not aware of what my young friend had been doing on deck all that morning, but the hands he rubbed together vigorously were very red and imparted to me a chilly feeling by their mere aspect. He has remained the only banjoist of my acquaintance, and being also a younger son of a retired colonel, the poem of Mr. Kipling, by a strange aberration of associated ideas, always seems to me to have been written with an exclusive view to his person. When he did not play the banjo he loved to sit and look at it. He proceeded to this sentimental inspection, and after meditating a while over the strings under my silent scrutiny, inquired airily:

===Chinese===
是暖和。我在那只漏水的水龙头底下放了一只白铁罐之后，打开了蒸汽暖气——因为你也许不知道，蒸汽不漏的地方，水倒会漏。我说不上我那位年轻朋友那天早晨在甲板上干了些什么，只是他使劲搓着的那双手红得很，光看一眼就传给我一股寒意。他一直是我相识中唯一的班卓琴手，又是一位退休上校的小儿子，于是吉卜林先生的那首诗，由于联想的奇妙错位，在我眼里似乎从来就是专为他一个人写的。不弹班卓琴的时候，他爱坐着看它。他这就着手进行这番多情的检视，在我的默默注视下对着琴弦出了一会儿神，然后轻飘飘地问：

===Original===
“What are you always scribbling there, if it’s fair to ask?”

===Chinese===
「如果这话问得的话——你老在那儿涂涂写写些什么呢？」

===Original===
It was a fair enough question, but I did not answer him, and simply turned the pad over with a movement of instinctive secrecy: I could not have told him he had put to flight the psychology of Nina Almayer, her opening speech of the tenth chapter and the words of Mrs. Almayer’s wisdom which were to follow in the ominous oncoming of a tropical night. I could not have told him that Nina had said: “It has set at last.” He would have been extremely surprised and perhaps have dropped his precious banjo. Neither could I have told him that the sun of my seagoing was setting too, even as I wrote the words expressing the impatience of passionate youth bent on its desire. I did not know this myself, and it is safe to say he would not have cared, though he was an excellent young fellow and treated me with more deference than, in our relative positions, I was strictly entitled to.

===Chinese===
这问题原也够正当的，可我没有答他，只凭一种本能的隐秘劲儿把稿纸翻了过去：我没法告诉他，他吓跑的是妮娜·阿尔迈耶的心理，是第十章的开场白，还有热带之夜不祥地将至时随后要说的阿尔迈耶太太的智语。我没法告诉他，妮娜说了：「它终于落下去了。」他会大吃一惊，说不定还要摔了他那宝贝班卓琴。我也没法告诉他，我海上生涯的太阳也在下沉——就在我写下这些字、写下热切的青春一心奔赴心愿时的那份焦灼的时候。这一点连我自己都不知道；而且可以放心说一句，他也不会在意，虽然他是个极好的年轻人，待我也比照我们彼此的职位、比我名分上应得的还要客气三分。

===Original===
He lowered a tender gaze on his banjo and I went on looking through the porthole. The round opening framed in its brass rim a fragment of the quays, with a row of casks ranged on the frozen ground and the tail-end of a great cart. A red-nosed carter in a blouse and a woollen nightcap leaned against the wheel. An idle, strolling customhouse guard, belted over his blue capote, had the air of being depressed by exposure to the weather and the monotony of official existence. The background of grimy houses found a place in the picture framed by my porthole, across a wide stretch of paved quay, brown with frozen mud. The colouring was sombre, and the most conspicuous feature was a little café with curtained windows and a shabby front of white woodwork, corresponding with the squalor of these poorer quarters bordering the river. We had been shifted down there from another berth in the neighbourhood of the Opera House, where that same porthole gave me a view of quite another sort of café﻿—the best in the town, I believe, and the very one where the worthy Bovary and his wife, the romantic daughter of old Père Renault, had some refreshment after the memorable performance of an opera which was the tragic story of Lucia di Lammermoor in a setting of light music.

===Chinese===
他朝班卓琴垂下温柔的目光，我则继续望着舷窗外。那镶着黄铜圈的圆孔，框住了一段码头的碎片：冻硬的地上一排酒桶，还有一辆大车的尾巴。一个鼻子通红的车夫，穿罩衫、戴绒线睡帽，倚着车轮。一个闲逛的海关警卫，蓝斗篷外束着腰带，一副被风吹日晒和公职生活的单调压得垂头丧气的神气。隔着一大片铺石码头——冻泥把它染成棕褐色——几排灰蒙蒙的房屋也在这幅舷窗框出的图画里占了一席。色调是晦暗的，最显眼的去处是一家小咖啡馆：窗上挂着帘子，白木门面寒酸破落，正与河岸这些贫苦街区的龌龊相称。我们是从歌剧院附近另一个泊位被挪到这边来的；在那边，同一扇舷窗给我看的是完全另一种咖啡馆——全城最好的，我相信——而且恰恰就是那一家：可敬的包法利和他的妻子、老雷诺爹的浪漫女儿，在那场令人难忘的歌剧演出之后进去用过点心——那出戏，是把《拉美莫尔的露琪亚》的悲惨故事安在轻音乐布景里演出来的。

===Original===
I could recall no more the hallucination of the Eastern Archipelago which I certainly hoped to see again. The story of *Almayer’s Folly* got put away under the pillow for that day. I do not know that I had any occupation to keep me away from it; the truth of the matter is that on board that ship we were leading just then a contemplative life. I will not say anything of my privileged position. I was there “just to oblige,” as an actor of standing may take a small part in the benefit performance of a friend.

===Chinese===
东印度群岛的幻视，我再也召不回来了——虽然我确实盼望再见它一次。《阿尔迈耶的愚蠢》的故事，那一天被收起来塞到了枕头底下。我说不上有什么事务让我离开它；实情是，在那条船上，我们当时过的是一种静观的生活。我那优越的位置就不去说它了。我在那里「只是为了帮个忙」，正如一位有身份的演员会在朋友的义演里接一个配角。

===Original===
As far as my feelings were concerned I did not wish to be in that steamer at that time and in those circumstances. And perhaps I was not even wanted there in the usual sense in which a ship “wants” an officer. It was the first and last instance in my sea life when I served shipowners who have remained completely shadowy to my apprehension. I do not mean this for the well-known firm of London ship-brokers which had chartered the ship to the, I will not say short-lived, but ephemeral Franco-Canadian Transport Company. A death leaves something behind, but there was never anything tangible left from the F.C.T.C. It flourished no longer than roses live, and unlike the roses it blossomed in the dead of winter, emitted a sort of faint perfume of adventure, and died before spring set in. But indubitably it was a company, it had even a house-flag, all white with the letters F.C.T.C. artfully tangled up in a complicated monogram. We flew it at our mainmast head, and now I have come to the conclusion that it was the only flag of its kind in existence. All the same we on board, for many days, had the impression of being a unit of a large fleet with fortnightly departures for Montreal and Quebec as advertised in pamphlets and prospectuses which came aboard in a large package in Victoria Dock, London, just before we started for Rouen, France. And in the shadowy life of the F.C.T.C. lies the secret of that, my last employment in my calling, which in a remote sense interrupted the rhythmical development of Nina Almayer’s story.

===Chinese===
就我的感情而言，我当时并不愿意在那种时刻、那种境况里待在那条汽轮上。而且或许，按一条船「需要」一位驾驶员的寻常意义说，那里甚至并不需要我。这是我海上生涯里头一遭、也是末一遭：为我所服务的船东，在我的认识里始终是全然影影绰绰的。我这么说，并不是指那家有名声的伦敦船舶经纪行——船是他们租给那家公司的；我不说它短命，只说它朝生暮死：法加轮运公司。一场死亡总还留下点什么，可法加轮运公司从来没有留下任何有形之物。它的兴旺盛开，不比玫瑰的花期更长；而与玫瑰不同的是，它开在隆冬时节，散出一缕淡淡的冒险的芬芳，没等春天来临就死掉了。但无可置疑，它是一家公司，甚至有一面公司旗：旗面全白，把 F.C.T.C. 几个字母巧妙地纠缠成一个繁复的花押。我们把旗挂上主桅顶；如今我得出结论：那是世上独一份的这种旗子。尽管如此，一连许多天，船上我们这些人都觉得自己是一支大舰队的一个单位——两周一班开往蒙特利尔和魁北克，正如小册子和招商简章所宣传的那样；那一大包册子，是我们在伦敦维多利亚船坞启程赴法国鲁昂之前不久收上船的。而在我这行当里这最后一段差事的秘密——从一种迢远的意义上说，正是它打断了妮娜·阿尔迈耶故事那合于节奏的展开——就藏在法加轮运公司影影绰绰的一生里。
===Original===
The then secretary of the London Shipmasters’ Society, with its modest rooms in Fenchurch Street, was a man of indefatigable activity and the greatest devotion to his task. He is responsible for what was my last association with a ship. I call it that because it can hardly be called a seagoing experience. Dear Captain Froud﻿—it is impossible not to pay him the tribute of affectionate familiarity at this distance of years﻿—had very sound views as to the advancement of knowledge and status for the whole body of the officers of the mercantile marine. He organised for us courses of professional lectures, St. John ambulance classes, corresponded industriously with public bodies and members of Parliament on subjects touching the interests of the service; and as to the oncoming of some inquiry or commission relating to matters of the sea and to the work of seamen, it was a perfect godsend to his need of exerting himself on our corporate behalf. Together with this high sense of his official duties he had in him a vein of personal kindness, a strong disposition to do what good he could to the individual members of that craft of which in his time he had been a very excellent master. And what greater kindness can one do to a seaman than to put him in the way of employment? Captain Froud did not see why the Shipmasters’ Society, besides its general guardianship of our interests, should not be unofficially an employment agency of the very highest class.

===Chinese===
伦敦船长协会——它的几间屋子在芬乔奇街，再朴素不过——当时的秘书，是个对自己的任务不知疲倦、极度忠心的人。我与船的最后一段瓜葛，就是他促成的。我这样称呼它，是因为那实在算不上一段出海的经历。亲爱的弗劳德船长——隔了这么多年月，不这样亲热地称呼他是不可能的——对提高整个商船队军官阶层的知识与地位，有着十分正当的看法。他为我们组织职业讲座、圣约翰急救课程，就关系到这一行利益的事务，同公共机构和议员们勤勉通信；至于说有什么关于海事和海员工作的调查或委员会要来，那简直是天赐良机，正好满足他为我们这个群体出力的需要。与这份职务上的高度责任感并存的，是他身上一脉为人的慈和，一种强烈的性情，要为他那个行会的每一个成员——他当年曾是其中极出色的师傅——尽力做点好事。而对一个海员，还能有什么比给他谋一条差事更大的善意呢？弗劳德船长看不出，船长协会除了对我们的利益作一般守护之外，为什么不能非正式地兼做一个最高级的职业介绍所。

===Original===
“I am trying to persuade all our great ship-owning firms to come to us for their men. There is nothing of a trade-union spirit about our society, and I really don’t see why they should not,” he said once to me. “I am always telling the captains, too, that all things being equal they ought to give preference to the members of the society. In my position I can generally find for them what they want amongst our members or our associate members.”

===Chinese===
「我正在劝说我们所有的大船东行号都上我们这儿来要人。我们协会没有一点工会的气味，我实在看不出他们为什么不来，」有一回他对我说。「我还一直跟船长们讲，条件相当的时候，他们理应优先用协会的会员。以我的位置，我多半能在我们的会员或联系会员里，替他们找到想要的人。」

===Original===
In my wanderings about London from West to East and back again (I was very idle then), the two little rooms in Fenchurch Street were a sort of resting-place where my spirit, hankering after the sea, could feel itself nearer to the ships, the men, and the life of its choice﻿—nearer there than on any other spot of the solid earth. This resting-place used to be, at about five o’clock in the afternoon, full of men and tobacco smoke, but Captain Froud had the smaller room to himself and there he granted private interviews, whose principal motive was to render service. Thus, one murky November afternoon he beckoned me in with a crooked finger and that peculiar glance above his spectacles which is perhaps my strongest physical recollection of the man.

===Chinese===
我在伦敦从西头逛到东头、又逛回来的时候（那时我很闲），芬乔奇街那两间小屋便是一处歇脚的地方；我那眷恋着大海的心魂，在那里能觉得自己离船、离人、离它选定的生活更近——比在坚实大地的任何一点上都近。这处歇脚地，每到下午五点钟上下总是挤满了人，满是烟草的烟；但弗劳德船长有那间小屋子归他自己用，他在里面接待私人来访，而那些接待的主旨就是帮人办事。于是，一个阴沉沉的十一月午后，他朝我勾了勾手指，眼睛从眼镜上方那样特别地一瞟——那也许是我对这个人最强的一件形体上的记忆——把我叫了进去。

===Original===
“I have had in here a shipmaster, this morning,” he said, getting back to his desk and motioning me to a chair, “who is in want of an officer. It’s for a steamship. You know, nothing pleases me more than to be asked, but unfortunately I do not quite see my way.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「今天上午我这儿来过一位船长，」他说着回到桌后，示意我坐到椅子上，「他缺一位驾驶员。是条汽船。你知道，没有什么事比人家来求我更叫我高兴的了，可惜不大凑巧，我看不出有什么办法。……」

===Original===
As the outer room was full of men I cast a wondering glance at the closed door, but he shook his head.

===Chinese===
外间满是人的，我朝那扇关着的门投去疑问的一瞥，他却摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Oh, yes, I should be only too glad to get that berth for one of them. But the fact of the matter is, the captain of that ship wants an officer who can speak French fluently, and that’s not so easy to find. I do not know anybody myself but you. It’s a second officer’s berth and, of course, you would not care﻿ ﻿… would you now? I know that it isn’t what you are looking for.”

===Chinese===
「噢，是啊，能把那个职位给他们中间哪一个，我自然再乐意不过。可事实是，那条船的船长要一位能说流利法语的驾驶员，这就不好找了。我自己认识的人里，除了你没有一个。那是二副的职位，当然啦，你不会愿意……你会愿意吗？我知道这不是你要找的东西。」

===Original===
It was not. I had given myself up to the idleness of a haunted man who looks for nothing but words wherein to capture his visions. But I admit that outwardly I resembled sufficiently a man who could make a second officer for a steamer chartered by a French company. I showed no sign of being haunted by the fate of Nina and by the murmurs of tropical forests; and even my intimate intercourse with Almayer (a person of weak character) had not put a visible mark upon my features. For many years he and the world of his story had been the companions of my imagination without, I hope, impairing my ability to deal with the realities of sea life. I had had the man and his surroundings with me ever since my return from the eastern waters, some four years before the day of which I speak.

===Chinese===
确实不是。我那时已沉溺于一个鬼魂缠身之人的闲散里，别的什么都不找，只找能捉住自己幻象的字句。但我承认，外表上我十足像是一个能当法国公司租的汽船二副的人。我一点没有显出被妮娜的命运、被热带森林的絮语纠缠的样子；甚至与阿尔迈耶（一个性格软弱的人）的亲密交往，也没在我脸上留下看得见的印记。多年来，他和他的故事世界一直是我想象力的伴侣，而并未——我希望——损害我应付海上生活现实事务的能力。这个人连同他的环境，是打我从东方水域回来后就跟在我身边的；那距我说话的这一天，约有四个年头了。
===Original===
It was in the front sitting-room of furnished apartments in a Pimlico square that they first began to live again with a vividness and poignancy quite foreign to our former real intercourse. I had been treating myself to a long stay on shore, and in the necessity of occupying my mornings, Almayer (that old acquaintance) came nobly to the rescue. Before long, as was only proper, his wife and daughter joined him round my table, and then the rest of that Pantai band came full of words and gestures. Unknown to my respectable landlady, it was my practice directly after my breakfast to hold animated receptions of Malays, Arabs and half-castes. They did not clamour aloud for my attention. They came with a silent and irresistible appeal﻿—and the appeal, I affirm here, was not to my self-love or my vanity. It seems now to have had a moral character, for why should the memory of these beings, seen in their obscure sunbathed existence, demand to express itself in the shape of a novel, except on the ground of that mysterious fellowship which unites in a community of hopes and fears all the dwellers on this earth?

===Chinese===
是在皮姆利科一处广场的带家具公寓的前客厅里，他们才又开始活了起来，那份鲜活与锥心，全然是我们从前真实交往中所没有的。我那时正纵容自己长期待在岸上，为了有事打发上午，阿尔迈耶（那位旧相识）慨然前来救场。不久——这也是理当如此——他的妻子和女儿也到他身边、围着我的桌子入座了，随后帕泰（Pantai）那一伙的其余人等也来了，满肚子的话，满身的手势。我那位可敬的房东太太一无所知：我的习惯是早餐一过，就举行热烈的招待会，接待马来人、阿拉伯人和混血儿。他们不为引起我的注意而喧嚷。他们带来的是一种无言而不可抗拒的吁求——而这吁求，我在此申明，并非诉诸我的自恋或虚荣。如今回想，它仿佛有一种道德的性质；因为，若不是凭着那种神秘的伙伴之情——它把大地上的一切居住者结合成一个忧惧与希望相通的共同体——这些生命——我在他们默默无闻而又洒满阳光的生存中所认识的生命——的记忆，为什么竟要求以一部小说的形式来表达自己呢？

===Original===
I did not receive my visitors with boisterous rapture as the bearers of any gifts of profit or fame. There was no vision of a printed book before me as I sat writing at that table, situated in a decayed part of Belgravia. After all these years, each leaving its evidence of slowly blackened pages, I can honestly say that it is a sentiment akin to piety which prompted me to render in words assembled with conscientious care the memory of things far distant and of men who had lived.

===Chinese===
我接待这些来访者，并没有大喜若狂，把他们当作带来名利之礼的人。我坐在那张桌子前写的时候，眼前并没有一本印成的书的幻象；那桌子坐落的地方，还算贝尔格莱维亚的一个败落地段。隔了这么多年，每一年都留下它的证据——渐渐发黑的纸页——我可以诚诚实实地说：促使我把远隔的事物、把生活过的人们的记忆，用一番出于良心关怀而集结起来的文字写出来的，是一种近乎虔敬的情感。

===Original===
But, coming back to Captain Froud and his fixed idea of never disappointing shipowners or ship-captains, it was not likely that I should fail him in his ambition﻿—to satisfy at a few hours’ notice the unusual demand for a French-speaking officer. He explained to me that the ship was chartered by a French company intending to establish a regular monthly line of sailings from Rouen, for the transport of French emigrants to Canada. But, frankly, this sort of thing did not interest me very much. I said gravely that if it were really a matter of keeping up the reputation of the Shipmasters’ Society, I would consider it. But the consideration was just for form’s sake. The next day I interviewed the Captain, and I believe we were impressed favourably with each other. He explained that his chief mate was an excellent man in every respect and that he could not think of dismissing him so as to give me the higher position; but that if I consented to come as second officer I would be given certain special advantages﻿—and so on.

===Chinese===
可是，回到弗劳德船长和他那从不让船东或船长失望的固执念头上：在他那桩抱负——在一纸几个小时的通知之内，满足对一位说法语的驾驶员的非常需求——上，我也不至于让他落空。他向我解释说，船是一家法国公司租的，那公司打算开辟一条自鲁昂按月一班的正规航线，把法国移民运往加拿大。可是老实说，这类事情我兴趣不大。我郑重地说，如果这真是关系到维护船长协会名誉的事，我可以考虑。不过那番考虑只是走个形式。第二天我会见了那位船长，我相信我们彼此的印象都不坏。他解释说，他的大副样样都好，他不能为了给我较高的位置而把人家辞掉；但如果我肯来当二副，会给我若干特别的优待——云云。

===Original===
I told him that if I came at all the rank really did not matter.

===Chinese===
我对他说，如果我真要去的话，职衔实在无所谓。

===Original===
“I am sure,” he insisted, “you will get on first rate with Mr. Paramor.”

===Chinese===
「我担保，」他坚持道，「你同帕拉莫尔先生会处得极好的。」

===Original===
I promised faithfully to stay for two trips at least, and it was in those circumstances that what was to be my last connection with a ship began. And after all there was not even one single trip. It may be that it was simply the fulfilment of a fate, of that written word on my forehead which apparently forbade me, through all my sea wanderings, ever to achieve the crossing of the Western Ocean﻿—using the words in that special sense in which sailors speak of Western Ocean trade, of Western Ocean packets, of Western Ocean hard cases. The new life attended closely upon the old, and the nine chapters of *Almayer’s Folly* went with me to the Victoria Dock, whence in a few days we started for Rouen. I won’t go so far as saying that the engaging of a man fated never to cross the Western Ocean was the absolute cause of the Franco-Canadian Transport Company’s failure to achieve even a single passage. It might have been that, of course; but the obvious, gross obstacle was clearly the want of money. Four hundred and sixty bunks for emigrants were put together in the ’tween decks by industrious carpenters while we lay in the Victoria Dock, but never an emigrant turned up in Rouen﻿—of which, being a humane person, I confess I was glad. Some gentlemen from Paris﻿—I think there were three of them, and one was said to be the Chairman﻿—turned up indeed and went from end to end of the ship, knocking their silk hats cruelly against the deck-beams. I attended them personally, and I can vouch for it that the interest they took in things was intelligent enough, though, obviously, they had never seen anything of the sort before. Their faces as they went ashore wore a cheerfully inconclusive expression. Notwithstanding that this inspecting ceremony was supposed to be a preliminary to immediate sailing, it was then, as they filed down our gangway, that I received the inward monition that no sailing within the meaning of our charter-party would ever take place.

===Chinese===
我忠实地许诺至少干两个航次；我同一条船的最后一段瓜葛，就是在这样的情形里开始的。而到头来，连一个航次也没有。这也许只是一个命途的兑现：我额头上写定的那行字，分明不许我在历次海上漂泊中完成对西大洋的横渡——「西大洋」这几个字，我取的是水手们谈西大洋航线、西大洋邮船、西大洋苦差时的那个特别意思。新的生活紧随着旧的而来，于是《阿尔迈耶的愚蠢》的九章稿子随我去了维多利亚船坞，没过几天，我们从那里启航去鲁昂。我不至于说，雇下我这个命中注定永不能横渡西大洋的人，就是法加轮运公司连一个航次也未能完成的绝对原因。当然，也许正是如此；但那道摆在眼前的、粗糙的障碍，分明是缺钱。我们停在维多利亚船坞的当儿，勤快的木匠们在中舱拼起了四百六十个移民铺位；可是在鲁昂，从来没有一个移民露过面——作为一个心肠不坏的人，我承认这是让我高兴的。倒是几位巴黎来的先生——记得是三位，据说其中一位是董事长——当真露了面，把船从头到尾走了一遍，丝礼帽撞在船舱横梁上，撞得可怜。我亲自陪着他们，可以担保他们对事物的兴趣足够在行，虽然他们显然从没见过这等东西。他们下船上岸时，脸上都带着一种快活而定不下案的神情。尽管这视察仪式被当作随即启航的先声，可就在他们鱼贯走下舷梯的时候，我心里得到一种内在的晓谕：我们租约意义上的启航，永远不会发生。

===Original===
It must be said that in less than three weeks a move took place. When we first arrived we had been taken up with much ceremony well towards the centre of the town, and, all the street corners being placarded with the tricolour posters announcing the birth of our company, the petit bourgeois with his wife and family made a Sunday holiday from the inspection of the ship. I was always in evidence in my best uniform to give information as though I had been a Cook’s tourists’ interpreter, while our quartermasters reaped a harvest of small change from personally conducted parties. But when the move was made﻿—that move which carried us some mile and a half down the stream to be tied up to an altogether muddier and shabbier quay﻿—then indeed the desolation of solitude became our lot. It was a complete and soundless stagnation; for, as we had the ship ready for sea to the smallest detail, as the frost was hard and the days short, we were absolutely idle﻿—idle to the point of blushing with shame when the thought struck us that all the time our salaries went on. Young Cole was aggrieved because, as he said, we could not enjoy any sort of fun in the evening after loafing like this all day: even the banjo lost its charm since there was nothing to prevent his strumming on it all the time between the meals. The good Paramor﻿—he was really a most excellent fellow﻿—became unhappy as far as was possible to his cheery nature, till one dreary day I suggested out of sheer mischief, that he should employ the dormant energies of the crew in hauling both cables up on deck and turning them end for end.

===Chinese===
必须说一句：不出三个星期，挪窝的事就发生了。我们初到时，被煞有介事地带上去，一直带到快近城中心的地方；街角四处贴着三色海报，宣布我们公司的诞生，小市民们便带着妻儿，把参观这条船当成了星期日的节日消遣。我总是穿着我最好的制服到场答疑，活像库克旅行社雇的游客讲解员，而我们的舵手们则从亲自带队参观里捞了一阵子小钱。可等到挪了窝——那一挪，把我们顺流送下去一英里半，拴在一段泥泞得多、寒酸得多的码头上——荒凉与孤寂可就真的成了我们的份。那是一摊全然无声的停滞；因为船已样样备航、纤悉无遗，天又冻得硬、白日又短，我们就绝对地闲——闲到一想起来薪水照发，便羞得脸红。年轻的科尔满腹委屈，因为照他说，成天这么游荡之后，晚上连一点乐子也享受不成了：连班卓琴都失去了魅力，因为两顿饭之间反正随时可以弹，没有什么拦着他。好心的帕拉莫尔——他实在是个顶好的人——尽他那快活天性所能容许的程度，变得郁郁不乐；直到一个阴郁的日子，我纯粹出于促狭，提议他不妨把船员们蛰伏的精力派上用场，把两条锚链都拖上甲板，调个头、尾对尾重新铺放。

===Original===
For a moment Mr. Paramor was radiant. “Excellent idea!” but directly his face fell. “Why﻿ ﻿… Yes! But we can’t make that job last more than three days,” he muttered discontentedly. I don’t know how long he expected us to be stuck on the riverside outskirts of Rouen, but I know that the cables got hauled up and turned end for end according to my satanic suggestion, put down again, and their very existence utterly forgotten, I believe, before a French river pilot came on board to take our ship down, empty as she came, into the Havre roads. You may think that this state of forced idleness favoured some advance in the fortunes of Almayer and his daughter. Yet it was not so. As if it were some sort of evil spell, my banjoist cabin-mate’s interruption, as related above, had arrested them short at the point of that fateful sunset for many weeks together. It was always thus with this book, begun in ’89 and finished in ’94﻿—with that shortest of all the novels which it was to be my lot to write. Between its opening exclamation calling Almayer to his dinner in his wife’s voice and Abdullah’s (his enemy) mental reference to the God of Islam﻿—“The Merciful, the Compassionate”﻿—which closes the book, there were to come several long sea passages, a visit (to use the elevated phraseology suitable to the occasion) to the scenes (some of them) of my childhood and the realization of childhood’s vain words, expressing a lighthearted and romantic whim.

===Chinese===
有一刹那，帕拉莫尔先生容光焕发。「好主意！」可紧跟着他的脸就垮了。「呃……是呀！可这活儿至多撑不过三天，」他不满足地咕哝道。我不知道他指望我们在鲁昂的河郊野外困多久；但我知道，那两条锚链照我的撒旦式建议拖了上去、调了头，又放了下去，而它们的存在本身，我相信，早在一位法国引水员上船、把我们这条空着来的船再空着送下去、开进勒阿弗尔外港海面之前，就被忘了个一干二净。你也许以为，这种被迫的闲散，会助阿尔迈耶和他女儿的命运一臂之力。其实不然。仿佛中了什么邪祟，如上文所述，我那位班卓琴室友的打断，把他们钉死在那场决定性日落的一刻，一钉就是好几个星期。这本书从来就是这样：1889 年动笔，1894 年完稿——它是我命里注定要写的小说中最短的一部。从开篇那一声用他妻子嗓音唤阿尔迈耶来吃饭的呼喊，到阿卜杜拉（他的仇敌）心里对伊斯兰之神的念诵——「至仁至慈的主」，全书以此收束——这中间要来的是：好几段长长的海上航程，一次（借用适合这场合的崇高辞令来说）对我童年若干故地的造访，还有童年虚妄之言的应验——那几句话，说的原是一桩无忧无虑、浪漫入骨的奇想。

===Original===
It was in 1868, when nine years old or thereabouts, that while looking at a map of Africa of the time and putting my finger on the blank space then representing the unsolved mystery of that continent, I said to myself with absolute assurance and an amazing audacity which are no longer in my character now:

===Chinese===
那是 1868 年，我九岁上下，正看着当时的一幅非洲地图，手指按在那片空白上——它当时代表着那大陆未曾破解的谜——我以绝对的把握、以一种如今在我性格里已不复见的惊人的大胆，对自己说：
===Original===
“When I grow up I shall go *there*.”

===Chinese===
「等我长大了，我要到*那里*去。」

===Original===
And of course I thought no more about it till after a quarter of a century or so an opportunity offered to go there﻿—as if the sin of childish audacity were to be visited on my mature head. Yes. I did go there: *there* being the region of Stanley Falls which in ’68 was the blankest of blank spaces on the earth’s figured surface. And the MS. of *Almayer’s Folly*, carried about me as if it were a talisman or a treasure, went *there* too. That it ever came out of *there* seems a special dispensation of Providence; because a good many of my other properties, infinitely more valuable and useful to me, remained behind through unfortunate accidents of transportation. I call to mind, for instance, a specially awkward turn of the Congo between Kinchassa and Leopoldsville﻿—more particularly when one had to take it at night in a big canoe with only half the proper number of paddlers. I failed in being the second white man on record drowned at that interesting spot through the upsetting of a canoe. The first was a young Belgian officer, but the accident happened some months before my time, and he, too, I believe, was going home; not perhaps quite so ill as myself﻿—but still he was going home. I got round the turn more or less alive, though I was too sick to care whether I did or not, and, always with *Almayer’s Folly* amongst my diminishing baggage, I arrived at that delectable capital Boma, where before the departure of the steamer which was to take me home I had the time to wish myself dead over and over again with perfect sincerity. At that date there were in existence only seven chapters of *Almayer’s Folly*, but the chapter in my history which followed was that of a long, long illness and very dismal convalescence. Geneva, or more precisely the hydropathic establishment of Champel, is rendered forever famous by the termination of the eighth chapter in the history of Almayer’s decline and fall. The events of the ninth are inextricably mixed up with the details of the proper management of a waterside warehouse owned by a certain city firm whose name does not matter. But that work, undertaken to accustom myself again to the activities of a healthy existence, soon came to an end. The earth had nothing to hold me with for very long. And then that memorable story, like a cask of choice Madeira, got carried for three years to and fro upon the sea. Whether this treatment improved its flavour or not, of course I would not like to say. As far as appearance is concerned it certainly did nothing of the kind. The whole MS. acquired a faded look and an ancient, yellowish complexion. It became at last unreasonable to suppose that anything in the world would ever happen to Almayer and Nina. And yet something most unlikely to happen on the high seas was to wake them up from their state of suspended animation.

===Chinese===
而我自然再没想过这件事，直到四分之一世纪过后，一个到那里去的机会自己送上门来——仿佛童年轻率之罪，要报应在成年的我头上似的。是的。我确实到那里去了：那里，就是斯坦利瀑布地区；1868 年，它还是地球有图表面上最空白的一块空白。《阿尔迈耶的愚蠢》的手稿随身带着，仿佛一件护符或一件财宝，也跟着去了那里。它居然能从那里出来，似乎是上天特别的眷顾；因为我别的许多东西——对我远为有用、远为宝贵的东西——都因为运输途中的不幸事故留在后头了。我记得，比如说，刚果河在金沙萨与利奥波德维尔之间一段格外别扭的转弯——尤其当人不得不在夜里、乘一条只有半数桨手的大独木舟去闯它的时候。差一点，我就成了记录在案的、在那个有趣地点因独木舟倾覆而淹死的第二个白人。第一个是一位年轻的比利时军官，事故发生在我去之前的几个月；他呢，我相信，也是要回家；也许不像我病得那么厉害——但他也是要回家。我半死不活地把那个转弯闯了过去，虽然我病得顾不上自己闯没闯得过去；手稿《阿尔迈耶的愚蠢》一直夹在我越来越少的行李里，我到了那座美妙的都城博马。在那里，等着接我回家的汽船启程之前，我有的是时间，一次又一次、无比真诚地巴望自己死掉。在那个日子，《阿尔迈耶的愚蠢》只存在七章；但我个人历史里随后的那一章，是一场漫长的疾病和一场惨淡之极的康复。日内瓦——更确切地说，尚佩尔的水疗院——因阿尔迈耶败落史第八章的完成而永垂不朽了。第九章里的事件，则同一座滨水货栈经营管理的种种细节不可分解地搅在一起，货栈属于某家城市商行，名字无关紧要。可那件工作——为的是让自己重新习惯健康生存的活动——不久就结束了。大地再没有什么能长久拴住我。于是那部值得纪念的故事，像一桶上选的马德拉酒，在海上被搬来搬去三年。这般处置是否增进了它的风味，我自然不愿妄言。至于外观，它确实毫无长进。整部手稿得了副褪了色的面貌、一种古老的淡黄脸色。到后来，再设想世上会有什么事落到阿尔迈耶和妮娜头上，已属不近情理。然而，一件在大海上最不可能发生的事，竟要把他们从假死的状态里唤醒。

===Original===
What is it that Novalis says? “It is certain my conviction gains infinitely the moment another soul will believe in it.” And what is a novel if not a conviction of our fellow-men’s existence strong enough to take upon itself a form of imagined life clearer than reality and whose accumulated verisimilitude of selected episodes puts to shame the pride of documentary history? Providence which saved my MS. from the Congo rapids brought it to the knowledge of a helpful soul far out on the open sea. It would be on my part the greatest ingratitude ever to forget the sallow, sunken face and the deep-set, dark eyes of the young Cambridge man (he was a “passenger for his health” on board the good ship *Torrens* outward bound to Australia) who was the first reader of *Almayer’s Folly*﻿—the very first reader I ever had. “Would it bore you very much reading a MS. in a handwriting like mine?” I asked him one evening on a sudden impulse at the end of a longish conversation whose subject was Gibbon’s *History*. Jacques (that was his name) was sitting in my cabin one stormy dogwatch below, after bringing me a book to read from his own travelling store.

===Chinese===
诺瓦利斯是怎么说的？「我的信念，一旦有另一个灵魂肯相信，其分量便无限地增长——这是确定无疑的。」而一部小说，不正是对我们同类之存在的一种信念，强到足以亲自担起一种想象的生活——比现实更清晰的生活——而其所选片段累积起来的逼真，令文献史学的骄傲都相形见绌么？把我的手稿从刚果激流里救出的上天，又把它送到汪洋大海远处一个乐于助人的灵魂面前。倘若忘了那张灰黄的凹陷面孔、那双深陷的黑眼睛，那便是天下第一等的忘恩负义了——那剑桥青年（他是好船「托伦斯号」上「养病的乘客」，船正外驶澳大利亚）是《阿尔迈耶的愚蠢》的第一位读者——我平生拥有的第一位读者。「读一份我这样笔迹的手稿，会不会叫你烦得慌？」一天晚上，一场长谈的末尾，我一阵心血来潮问他；谈的是吉本的那部《历史》。雅克（那是他的名字）刚从他自己的行装里取了本书来给我读，趁着风暴之下的一个暮更，坐在我底下的舱房里。

===Original===
“Not at all,” he answered with his courteous intonation and a faint smile. As I pulled a drawer open his suddenly aroused curiosity gave him a watchful expression. I wonder what he expected to see. A poem, maybe. All that’s beyond guessing now. He was not a cold, but a calm man, still more subdued by disease﻿—a man of few words and of an unassuming modesty in general intercourse, but with something uncommon in the whole of his person which set him apart from the undistinguished lot of our sixty passengers. His eyes had a thoughtful, introspective look. In his attractive, reserved manner, and in a veiled, sympathetic voice, he asked:

===Chinese===
「一点也不，」他用他彬彬有礼的声调和一丝淡淡的微笑答道。我拉开一只抽屉，他蓦然被唤起的好奇给了他一副提防的神色。我不知道他以为会看见什么。也许是一首诗。如今都无从猜起了。他不是一个冷淡的人，而是一个平静的人，又被疾病压得更沉了——一个言语不多、日常交往中谦逊朴实的人，但他整个人的身上有一点不同寻常的东西，使他从我们六十名乘客那班无名的群体里脱出。他的眼睛有一种沉思的、内省的神色。以他那吸引人的、矜持的态度，用一种蒙着面纱似的、同情的声音，他问：

===Original===
“What is this?” “It is a sort of tale,” I answered with an effort. “It is not even finished yet. Nevertheless, I would like to know what you think of it.” He put the MS. in the breast-pocket of his jacket; I remember perfectly his thin brown fingers folding it lengthwise. “I will read it tomorrow,” he remarked, seizing the door-handle, and then, watching the roll of the ship for a propitious moment, he opened the door and was gone. In the moment of his exit I heard the sustained booming of the wind, the swish of the water on the decks of the *Torrens*, and the subdued, as if distant, roar of the rising sea. I noted the growing disquiet in the great restlessness of the ocean, and responded professionally to it with the thought that at eight o’clock, in another half-hour or so at the furthest, the topgallant sails would have to come off the ship.

===Chinese===
「这是什么？」「算是一部故事吧，」我费了点劲才答上来。「还没有写完呢。不过，我很想知道你觉得它怎么样。」他把稿子放进外衣的胸袋；我记得清清楚楚，他棕色的瘦手指把它对折了。「我明天读，」他说着抓住门把手，然后观望船的摇摆，等一个合适的时机，开了门就走。在他出门的一瞬，我听见风持续的轰鸣，海水扫过「托伦斯号」甲板的哗哗声，还有涨起的海那仿佛隔得很远的低吼。我觉出大海巨大的躁动中日渐增长的不安，并以职业的方式回应它，心想：八点钟——至多再过半个来小时——上桅帆就得从船上卸下了。

===Original===
Next day, but this time in the first dogwatch, Jacques entered my cabin. He had a thick, woollen muffler round his throat and the MS. was in his hand. He tendered it to me with a steady look but without a word. I took it in silence. He sat down on the couch and still said nothing. I opened and shut a drawer under my desk, on which a filled-up log-slate lay wide open in its wooden frame waiting to be copied neatly into the sort of book I was accustomed to write with care, the ship’s logbook. I turned my back squarely on the desk. And even then Jacques never offered a word. “Well, what do you say?” I asked at last. “Is it worth finishing?” This question expressed exactly the whole of my thoughts.

===Chinese===
第二天，不过这回是头一个暮更，雅克走进我的舱房。他喉咙上围着厚厚的毛围巾，手稿拿在手里。他以坚定的目光把稿子递给我，却没有一句话。我默默接了。他在长榻上坐下，仍旧一言不发。我打开又关上书桌底下的一个抽屉；书桌上，一块填满的航海日志板摊在木框里，等着被工整地誊进那种我一向用心书写的本子——船舶日志。我索性把背对着书桌。即便这样，雅克还是一言不发。「嗯，你觉得怎么样？」我终于问。「值得写完吗？」这个问题，不多不少，正是我全部的心思。

===Original===
“Distinctly,” he answered in his sedate, veiled voice, and then coughed a little.

===Chinese===
「当然值得，」他以他平稳而含蓄的声音答道，随后轻轻咳了两声。
===Original===
“Were you interested?” I inquired further, almost in a whisper.

===Chinese===
「你读得有兴趣么？」我进一步问，几乎是耳语。

===Original===
“Very much!”

===Chinese===
「兴趣大得很！」

===Original===
In a pause I went on meeting instinctively the heavy rolling of the ship, and Jacques put his feet upon the couch. The curtain of my bed-place swung to and fro as it were a punkah, the bulkhead lamp circled in its gimbals, and now and then the cabin door rattled slightly in the gusts of wind. It was in latitude 40 south, and nearly in the longitude of Greenwich, as far as I can remember, that these quiet rites of Almayer’s and Nina’s resurrection were taking place. In the prolonged silence it occurred to me that there was a good deal of retrospective writing in the story as far as it went. Was it intelligible in its action, I asked myself, as if already the storyteller were being born into the body of a seaman. But I heard on deck the whistle of the officer of the watch and remained on the alert to catch the order that was to follow this call to attention. It reached me as a faint, fierce shout to “Square the yards.” “Aha!” I thought to myself, “a westerly blow coming on.” Then I turned to my very first reader, who, alas! was not to live long enough to know the end of the tale.

===Chinese===
在一阵沉默里，我本能地继续对付着船沉重的横摇；雅克把双脚搁上了长榻。我铺位的帘子像一架布风扇似的荡来荡去，舱壁灯在它的平环里打转，舱门在阵阵狂风里不时轻轻作响。那是在南纬 40 度、差不多格林尼治的经度上——就我记忆所及——阿尔迈耶与妮娜复活的一场安静的仪式正在举行。在长久的静默中，我忽然想到：故事写到目前的章节，追述的成分颇多。它的行动线是明白的吗？我自问，仿佛说故事的人已在水手体内开始诞生。但甲板上我听见值班驾驶员的哨音，便竖起耳朵，等着那一声注意召唤之后的口令。口令传到我这里，是一声轻微而猛烈的喊叫：「把帆桁转正！」「啊哈，」我心想，「要刮西风了。」随后我转向我的第一位读者——唉！他将活不到知道故事结尾的那一天了。

===Original===
“Now let me ask you one more thing: Is the story quite clear to you as it stands?”

===Chinese===
「现在再问你一件事：就眼下写成的样子，故事你完全看得明白么？」
